- Phase 4, addendum 002 (status rhythm), how it is applied. Config: `status.maxGapMinutes`, `status.minGapMinutes`, `status.maxPictures` and `status.inlineMaxTries` are in `config/showcase.json`, are required by the startup validation in `src/config.ts`, and are read from there by the harness. Looks: `tools/lib/looks.ts` logs every still, sheet, crop page and comparison the harness writes (the hook is `writeRecord`, so no command can forget it) as a JPEG copy with a caption under `out/work/looks/`, newest 96 kept; frames of a sheet or a sequence are logged at most every 45 s. Long jobs: every render through `openSource` (film, clip, pack, measure, stress, budget) writes a look of the frame it is on when it starts and then every `min(status.minGapMinutes, status.maxGapMinutes / 2)` minutes, taken from the pixels it already grabbed or from the canvas, never by rendering again. `npm run status` no longer renders: it shows, per stop, the newest key frame beside the approved image (a comparison, or the newest hold or terrain frame set beside the approved image), then the newest other looks; with no stop the newest looks since the last status, then the kept whole film sheets; it never returns empty. `--render` keeps the old behavior of addendum 001 (both comparisons and a dive sheet at the review tier). It writes the pictures to `out/stills/status/`, the packed file for the inline grid, one line into the Session log, the review page from the build as it stands (`tools/review.ts --no-build`), and with `--push` commits and pushes. `npm run status -- --due` says how long ago the last status was; it is asked at every poll. Supporting agents: `npm run looks -- --push --folder ID` writes the newest eight looks as one commit to the branch `agent/ID-looks` with git plumbing (the agent's own branch and working tree are not touched); `npm run looks -- --fetch` reads every such branch into `out/work/looks/remote/` without merging; `npm run looks -- --add FILE --label TEXT` logs any picture an agent is looking at (a painted map, a fit overlay). With eight stops in work at once and four pictures per status, each status shows the stops with the newest looks and names the others in its text.
- Phase 4, one change to the shared engine before the eight stops start, so that a plan change at one stop no longer needs a shared file written again. Before: `src/space/skyframe.json` stored the sky frame, the terrain sky turn and the patch aims as numbers that followed from the plans of every stop up to Mars, a test failed whenever a plan moved, and a tool wrote them again. With eight agents changing eight plans at once that file would conflict at every merge and change the engine hash each time. Now `FilmDirector` computes the same values from the stored arguments and the plans when it starts (`aimSky()` in `src/director/film.ts`, the aim math moved to `src/space/aim.ts`), rounded to seven decimals exactly as the tool rounded them; `skyframe.json` keeps only the arguments, the grade, the band, the stars and the two patch aims of the stop the sky is fitted to (they do not depend on any plan). New for the other stops: every stop gets an orbit patch aim on the view of its judged hold frame, a terrain patch aim on the view of its judged terrain frame, and may ask for a sky turn in its terrain view with `skyAim.terrain` in its `plan.ts` (band position, tilt and lift in degrees, as Mars uses). The engine sets each sky patch's aim from these when it draws. Checked: the computed frame, turn and both Mars patch aims equal the stored numbers digit for digit (`out/work/an` check, values in the commit), tests 24 green.
- Phase 4, how the eight stops are built. One supporting agent per stop, each on its own VM, all eight at once, in the order of `buildOrderAfterLookLock` when it comes to rounds, finishing and publishing. Each agent owns `src/stops/<id>/`, `public/assets/<id>/` and `assets-src/<id>/`, pushes to `agent/<id>`, pushes its newest looks to `agent/<id>-looks` at least every `status.maxGapMinutes`, and keeps its own notebook in `assets-src/<id>/NOTES.md`. Their standing instructions are `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md`, in the repo so an agent can read them again after a compaction. What a stop needs beyond the shared modules (rings, cloud layers, plasma loops) lives in the stop's own folder, so it is hashed with the stop and cannot change another stop. A change that only the shared engine can carry arrives as `assets-src/<id>/engine.patch` with a note; the Builder applies it to `main` only if it is additive and off by default, and checks Mars frame 2325 and frame 2580 byte for byte after it. The Builder integrates each stop with `git checkout origin/agent/<id> -- <its three folders>`, never by merging the branch, then runs the machine checks, the measures, the pack and the critics, and sends the issues back to the stop's agent. Disk budgets given to the agents: 150 MB for a stop's `full` set and 40 MB for its `web` set, so nine stops stay inside `repo.maxTreeGb` and the published site inside its 500 MB.
- Phase 4, the engine hash. Adding the `status` block to `config/showcase.json` and moving the sky aims into the director change the engine hash from 73b844358c466906 to cae3193a3821716c (the config and `src/space` are inside the hash). The Mars hash is unchanged (353338cc5c905af5). Freshness check as `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` asks, done as a byte comparison instead of by eye: the judged Mars `hold` frame 2325 and `terrain` frame 2580 were rendered at the `final` tier from the new build and have the same sha256 as the stills of the judged build (9895a050... and a7e1670a..., baselines kept under `out/work/baseline-73b8/`). So Mars shows exactly what was approved, its kept stills, comparison images and clip stay its evidence, and no Mars round is run. `npm run keep` asks for records of the current build, so Mars's pictures are rendered again only in Phase 6 from the frozen build. Added at 02:51 UTC: the judged `dive` frame 2427 and `rise` frame 2726 were rendered the same way and are byte-identical too (sha256 0a022282 and 79352ec5), so all four judged Mars frames are unchanged by the new engine hash.
- Phase 4, a Phase 5 module started early. The asteroid belt of `mars.travel` (Phase 5 step 2) is a separate module that needs no finished stop but Mars, so a supporting agent builds it now on `agent/belt` in its own folders, with its engine hook kept as a patch. Nothing of it reaches `main` before Phase 5 starts, so the engine hash the stops are built on does not move for it. The phase order is unchanged: Phase 5's steps, rounds and gate run after the Phase 4 gate.
- Phase 4, the published folder. The site has a size limit and nine stops with both texture sets, nine clips and the review page would pass it. `npm run site` (`tools/site.ts`) assembles `out/work/site/` from `dist/` after `npm run review`: everything but the stops' `full` sets, with the review films encoded smaller (clips 1280 wide, long films 960 wide, cache in `out/work/site-cache/`), prints the total and fails over its limit (480 MB, under the 500 MB the site allows). Publishing uses `out/work/site`. The `full` sets stay in the repository and in every render. Each stop's `web` set is at most 35 MB, told to all eight agents. Phase 5 to do, with the other engine changes because it moves the engine hash: the app falls back to the `web` set when a `full` set is not shipped, so `?mode=export` on the published site still renders (today `src/main.ts` picks `full` whenever the mode is offline).
- Phase 5 to do, collected while Phase 4 runs (not open items of a round, a work list). (1) `web` set fallback for offline renders on the published site. (2) Dust direction in `travel`: the belt agent's probe says the travel dust offset moves the motes toward the next body (flow direction dot the direction to Jupiter is +0.987 in `mars.travel`), which reads as flying backward; check it on a contact sheet and turn it so motes stream from the next body past the lens. (3) The belt module from `agent/belt` with its hook.
- Addendum 003 (planet posts), read at 03:41 UTC on 2026-11-21 while Phase 4 runs, how it is applied. Nothing about the film, the clips, the rounds or Mars changes. The skill: looked for once in the workspace's skill list and found, `org:synthetic-query-typing` (the interface recreated in code, the prompt typed); it is used as it is, and its font and source are not copied into the repo. A supporting agent (`intro_builder`, branch `agent/posts`, folder `assets-src/posts/`) builds the nine intros now, with `tools/post.ts` (`npm run post`, with `--check` and `--sheets`) and `npm run keep -- --posts`; the intros reach the Builder as attachments and are committed only in Phase 6 under `out/posts/`. The `posts` block of the config goes onto main with the next engine batch, because the config is inside the engine hash, followed by the byte check on Mars. The first intro frame is shown inline in a status once it exists. Phase 6 gets: `npm run post` for all nine after step 7, then `npm run keep -- --posts`; the cue times of each post in `cut/CUES.md`; where the posts are in `README.md`, with a line saying the interface in the intros is a recreation and no real query was sent; the nine posts first on the review page; a remade clip gets its post joined again; the one text check with one fresh agent and at most `posts.checkMaxFixes` fix passes (not a round); the five QA items; the `Posts:` line in the BUILD COMPLETE block.
- Phase 4, engine batch 1 (04:07 UTC on 2026-11-21). Three things went onto main together so that the stops merge main once. (a) Saturn's engine request 1: an optional `weight(time, view)` on a sky patch, multiplied into the patch weight; off unless a stop sets it. (b) The `posts` block of addendum 003 in `config/showcase.json`, checked in `src/config.ts` like the `status` block (a prompt for every stop, the read hold inside the intro length). (c) The profiler's changes from `agent/engine-perf`: star points are drawn only where they can touch the frame, in offline capture only (the loop over 3,465,000 points costs 44 to 67 ms per call in a browser, so the interactive app keeps drawing all points as before), and the band, patch and glare work of the sky pass runs only where it has weight (the software rasterizer does not skip an `if` block but does skip the body of a `for` loop). Measured by the profiler on 4 cores at the `final` tier, before and after: hold 72.2 to 57.0 s, terrain 92.5 to 58.9 s, dive 236.5 to 179.7 s, rise 250.0 to 87.5 s per frame. Engine hash: cae3193a3821716c, then 4b61c4b2b8c348cd with (a) and (b), then 434d0a5e367a62fd with (c). The Mars hash is unchanged (353338cc5c905af5). Freshness of Mars, checked by the Builder through the harness and not taken from the agent: with (a) and (b) frames 2325 and 2580, and with all three the four judged frames 2325, 2580, 2427 and 2726, rendered at the `final` tier, have the same sha256 as the stills of the judged build (9895a050, a7e1670a, 0a022282, 79352ec5). So Mars shows exactly what was approved and no Mars round is run. One point is open with the profiler: inside the patch loop the texture reads use implicit derivatives, which a hardware driver may get wrong on the edge pixels of a patch; it tries `textureGrad` and commits it only if the four hashes stay identical.
- R8 scoring in the round tool: an answer counts as correct only when the first body it names is the stop ("Phobos. Mars behind" no longer counts as Mars). The Builder still reads every answer and can override a vote by hand.
- Frame times at the joints of a joined video (found by the intro agent, 04:10 UTC). `concatCopy` joined parts without saying how long each part is, so ffmpeg took each part's length from its file header, which is rounded to a millisecond. After a part whose frame count is not a multiple of 3 the next frame came 5 ticks of 15360 early: the committed Mars clip has two such steps inside its rise (frame count right, 495; length 16.499349 s instead of 16.5 s; nothing visible). Fixed in `tools/lib/encode.ts`: each part is listed with its exact length (frames over frame rate). Tested on parts of 5, 4 and 66 frames: every step is 512 ticks and the frame rate reads 30/1. The Mars clip is joined again with the other clips in Phase 6 from the frozen build; until then the kept one stays as it is.

- Phase 4, render workers (04:20 UTC on 2026-11-21). Eight supporting agents, `render_01` to `render_08`, each on its own VM with 4 cores and about 16 GB free, started before the first stop reaches its rounds so that no round waits for a machine. Their whole brief is `assets-src/shared/RENDER-WORKER.md`: they render the commands of a job at a named commit and send the files back, nothing else. Setup proof: each rendered Mars frame 2325 at the draft tier and reported the first 16 characters of its sha256, `fd128b1a999f76fa`, the same as the Builder's VM (one worker mistyped it once and corrected it; its first job is compared byte by byte here like every other). More workers are started for the sandbox master in Phase 6.

On the branch `builder/batch2` (e05c153): the profiler's change 4 (the sky and the stars are not shaded under opaque objects in capture mode) and its star point change (c3f52bd), plus Mercury's engine request 1, an optional `terrain.rollDeg` in the move parameters (`src/director/moves.ts`, off when unset: the approved Mercury close image has its horizon tilted 11.3 degrees and the moves assumed a level terrain image). Build and 28 tests pass here; the candidate's engine hash is 05c29d3a1588e95f, the Mars hash is unchanged. The four Mars frames at the final tier are rendered by a render worker at that commit and compared here byte by byte with the baselines before anything reaches main. From now on the byte checks of engine changes run on a worker (4 cores) and the comparison runs here, so the Builder's VM stays free for rounds.

- Phase 4, engine batch 2 is on main (04:34 UTC on 2026-11-21). In it: the profiler's change 4 (in capture mode the sky pass and the star points are not shaded under opaque objects: the opaque part of the world is drawn first, the alpha of the sample target is read back, and the sky is drawn through a scissor over 32 px tiles that still hold an uncovered pixel), its change 5 (star points that lie wholly on covered tiles are not drawn), and Mercury's engine request 1 (`terrain.rollDeg`, off when unset). Checked before the merge: build and 28 tests green here; Mars frames 2325, 2580, 2427 and 2726 rendered at the final tier by `render_01` at the candidate commit (e05c153) are byte for byte the kept baselines, and their records carry the candidate's engine hash. The engine hash goes from 434d0a5e367a62fd to 05c29d3a1588e95f; the Mars hash is unchanged (353338cc5c905af5). Seconds per final frame on a 4 core VM (hold, terrain, dive, rise): 57.0, 58.9, 179.7, 87.5 before; 45.1, 60.7, 115.5, 80.4 now (the worker's times); at the start of Phase 4 they were 72.2, 92.5, 236.5, 250.0. Not in the batch: `textureGrad` in `patchTexel` (the profiler measured it slower on the software rasterizer and is trying a cheaper exact form).

- Phase 4, the sky aim per stop (decided 04:45 UTC on 2026-11-21, on main with engine batch 3). The hazard: each stop's world rotation follows from the stop before it (`place()` in `src/director/film.ts` turns a stop about the one Sun direction by an angle that comes from the previous stop's `travel.turnDeg` and `side` and from its exit pose), and the one sky is anchored to the Mars hold. So a plan change at one stop moved the fixed sky behind every stop after it, and a sky patch whose rim was matched to the sky around it would no longer match. With eight agents changing eight plans at once that would happen at every merge. The rule that stays: `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 5, "The sky keeps one orientation for the whole film. It turns only because the camera turns.", applied with the Phase 3 precedent that the sky may change its turn only under a cover, where no frame shows it (Mars's terrain sky does this since round 4). Built: an optional `skyAim: { orbit: [along, tilt, lift] }` in a stop's plan, the sky direction and roll the camera sees at the judged `hold` frame. A stop's aim holds from the rise of the stop before it (after that rise's cover), through that travel and its own hold, to its own dive cover; the terrain view uses `skyAim.terrain` or keeps the orbit turn; a stop's rise (orbit side) and travel already carry the next stop's turn; the ending keeps Neptune's. Three special cases: Jupiter's orbit aim is ignored, because its arrival shares the sky with the approved Mars rise, so Jupiter's sky follows only from Mars and its own plan; the Sun's hold shares its sky with Mercury's arrival (no cover lies between), so Mercury's aim decides and the Sun's is used only while Mercury has none; a stop without an aim carries the turn of the stop before it. With no aim declared nothing changes, which the byte check of batch 3 shows. `npm run -s aim [-- --stop ID]` (`tools/aim.ts`) prints each stop's present aims as the line to paste and what its plan sets. Tested here at the draft tier: pasting the printed aim leaves the hold frame pixel identical; a Jupiter aim is ignored; a Saturn aim changes the Saturn hold and the Jupiter rise frame 3296 and nothing else. A first variant that blended the turn during the travel passed its byte check and was dropped, because it turned the sky in view. Told to the stop agents in `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md`, section "Sky aim".

The profiler flagged this one as not exact in principle (at most one code value on an isolated pixel, none found in its tests); taken because the four judged Mars frames keep their bytes with it and it saves 5 to 26 percent per frame; if a later byte check on any stop shows a difference, it is the first thing to take out; (d) the asteroid belt of `mars.travel` from `agent/belt` (c377e83: `src/space/belt/`, `public/assets/shared/belt/` 34 MB, `assets-src/belt/`) with its engine hook, five lines in `src/core/engine.ts`. This changes the earlier decision that nothing of the belt reaches main before Phase 5: the engine hash moves for the sky aim anyway, the belt draws nothing outside frames 2767 to 2801, and one batch now saves a hash change and a round of merges for eight agents later. Phase 5 step 2 still happens in Phase 5: the belt is judged there with the travel it sits in. - Phase 4, the sky chain for a stop with no cover (decided 05:12 UTC on 2026-11-21, on main with engine batch 4, 31b2947). The hazard, raised by the Saturn agent: Saturn has no cover (its plan returns 0 from `coverAmount()`; its two views change over with the sky on screen, in the dive and in the rise at frame 3844), and the engine gave the orbit part of a rise and the travel after it the next stop's sky turn. So the moment Uranus set a sky aim of its own, the sky would have jumped in view at frame 3844. Two ways out were weighed. Taken: a stop with no cover shares one sky with the stop after it, and the stop with no cover decides it, exactly as Jupiter already shares the sky of the approved Mars rise. The engine reads the case from the plan (`coverAmount` at the cover centre of the rise is 0), so nothing is named in code. Such a stop also keeps one sky across its own two views: a terrain aim in its plan is ignored. Not taken: turning the sky during the part of the travel where only sky is in view, because `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 5 keeps the sky from turning in view and that choice was already made once for the sky aim. What follows: the sky behind Uranus's hold depends on Saturn's aim, Saturn's exit pose, `saturn.travel` turn and side, and Uranus's own arrival and hold, and on nothing else; Uranus's own orbit aim is ignored; Uranus fits the rim of its hold sky patch last, after Saturn says its exit and travel are final; the travel agent may not change the turn or side of `saturn.travel`, the same rule as for `mars.travel`. Checked: with the Saturn and Uranus branches merged on the candidate, the sky turn is one value over frames 3390 to 4460 (probe of `shotAt` at every frame), `npm run -s aim` names the case for Saturn and Uranus; build and 28 tests green; Mars frames 2325, 2580, 2427 and 2726 rendered at the final tier by `render_01` at the candidate commit (fb3c31d, engine hash a7e2e4a9a2b7be97) are byte for byte the baseline (sha256 9895a050abe22d0c, a7e1670abf3e4a24, 0a022282c24d2285, 79352ec57fa5c00b), and frame 2782 keeps the bytes it had with batch 3 (453c2129c026490d). - Phase 4, the world pin (decided 06:10 UTC on 2026-11-21, on main with engine batch 5). The hazard: `place()` in `src/director/film.ts` builds the world outward from the Sun, so the frame of every stop is the product of every plan before it. Earth's plan now sets `light.sunDir`, which turns Earth's frame and with it the frames of Mars and of every stop after it. Mars is approved and its pictures must not move. Measured here before the fix, at the draft tier with `agent/earth` mirrored onto main: frame 2325 differs from the kept bytes in 4914 pixels (largest step 3 of 255, 2 pixels over 2), frame 2580 in 908 pixels (largest step 1). So the picture is the same to the eye and the difference is rounding only, but the byte check that guards Mars at every engine batch would have been lost from the first integration of Sun, Mercury, Venus or Earth onward. Two ways out were weighed. Not taken: move the world so that Mars sits at the origin with no turn; it is the cleaner code, but it changes the Mars bytes once and it puts Neptune about 40,900 units from the origin instead of about 24,000 today, which no stop agent has tested its shaders at. Taken: the world is built as before and then moved as one rigid body so that Mars sits bit for bit where it sits today (`src/director/world.json`: the stop, its rotation, its origin and the one Sun direction, written by `out/work/pinworld.ts` from main at e408d4c with JSON's round trip of doubles; `pin()` in `src/director/film.ts`). Every other stop keeps its place relative to Mars, so no picture changes except by rounding in stops that are not finished. Proof: `tests/world.test.ts` (four tests: the pinned frame and Sun equal the stored numbers exactly; the stored Sun is the one Mars is lit from; with the Sun directions of all four stops before Mars changed, every Mars camera, set frame, Sun direction, sky turn and the sky frame are equal bit for bit over the hold, dive, terrain and rise; every stop is still lit from the one Sun). Draft stills here: on main's plans 2325 and 2580 keep their sha256 (`fd128b1a999f76fa`, `7322ef9576f06ffb`), and they keep them with `agent/earth`, `agent/sun`, `agent/mercury` and `agent/venus` all mirrored in (`/home/user/workspace/work/marsfree.sh earth,sun,mercury,venus origin/builder/batch5`). What still moves by design: `mars.travel` (frames 2745 to 2819) depends on Jupiter's plan, as every travel depends on the stop it flies to; frame 2782 stays in the byte check only as a check of the engine between two batches with the same plans. The Mars byte check is run at each integration of a stop from now on.

- Phase 4, the vote reader reads an option's words (07:29 UTC on 2026-11-21, `letter_how` in `tools/round.py`). The fault, found by the harness agent: a prose answer was searched for a lone letter, so the article in "Probably a stock image" counted as vote (a), which is the passing vote. Now: a letter at the start or in brackets counts as before; otherwise the answer is matched against the words of the five options (stock, textbook, amateur, web demo, film or cinematic) and counts only when exactly one option is named; an answer that begins with the article and an option's first word ("a stock image", "a real-time web 3D demo") is read as that option; a lone "a" in prose no longer counts. Every vote not read from the start of the answer is printed by `close` in stop rounds too, to be checked and corrected with `--vote NAME=LETTER`. The jury text, the panels and the pass rule are unchanged. Closed rounds are not counted again.

The profiler is done (its message starting DONE at 08:03 UTC); `assets-src/shared/PERF.md` has all ten changes with seconds and sha256 before and after. Its whole run on the four judged Mars frames at the final tier, its figures: hold 72.2 to 33.0 s, terrain 92.5 to 40.7, dive 236.5 to 76.7, rise 250.0 to 63.7. No further engine change is taken in Phase 4 except a stop's request that a round needs.
- Phase 4, rounds that start on one build share one run of the machine checks (08:19 UTC on 2026-11-21). Why: a round start runs the build, the tests and the whole Phase 4 gate on this 2 core machine, about ten minutes with every set loaded for G15; five stops are ready at once and their rounds render from the same tree. `tools/round.py start` has two new options: `--defer` writes the round's line first as always, mirrors the stop and commits without the checks; `--with 6,7,8` on the last start of the group runs the checks once on the tree that holds every mirrored stop of the group (G15 over the finished stops and every stop of the group), and gives each round of the group the same commit, the same gate result and its own stop hash. Nothing is skipped: every round's renders come from a commit the gate ran on, a red gate stops every round of the group, and each round still counts from the moment its line is written.

- Phase 4, twelve render workers (08:22 UTC on 2026-11-21). `render_09` to `render_12` were added, each on its own VM with 4 cores and the brief `assets-src/shared/RENDER-WORKER.md`. Why: eight first rounds at seven jobs each on eight workers would have queued for over an hour; with twelve, every job of a round goes out at once. The one render process per VM rule holds on each.
- Phase 4, how the first rounds of the eight stops ran (2026-11-21). Rounds 6 (uranus), 7 (mercury), 8 (jupiter), 9 (neptune), 10 (sun) and 12 (venus) were judged on main at 47a7600, one run of the machine checks for the group. Rounds 11 (earth, READY at 08:25 UTC, last agent commit 6d658f0) and 13 (saturn, READY at 08:49 UTC, 630a4df) started at 09:08 UTC on main at ae28011 with a second run of the checks, green, engine hash a3d211c425dc0406. Saturn's measure job runs with `--no-cover saturn`, which counts every frame of the stop, the stricter reading. Each round is rendered as seven jobs on the workers (stills, views, dive, dive cover, rise, rise cover, measure), placed and packed on the Builder's VM with no rendering.
- Phase 4, critics are sent out about ten at a time (09:12 UTC on 2026-11-21). The workspace allows 30 running agents; eight stop agents and twelve workers leave about ten places. A first round needs 30 critics (4 with scores, 20 jurors for R2 with controls, 6 for R8 with controls; the Sun 31). A rolling sender starts the next critic as soon as a place is free and records each name it has sent, so no critic is sent twice and none is left out. No critic, juror or control was dropped; the texts are the pack's, word for word.
- Phase 4, engine batch 10 waits for the first rounds (2026-11-21). The belt agent reported DONE at 08:38 UTC (`agent/belt` 234fc04): the belt refit and its dust. It is built and byte checked on `builder/batch10` (ffe0a2d) and is merged only when every first round is packed, so that no round is judged on a tree that changes under it. Second rounds run on batch 10. The harness agent's third part (`agent/harness` 393cdff: `tools/master.py`, `tools/cues.ts`, `tools/gauntlet.py`) is merged at the same time.
- Phase 4, a sky patch padded by mirroring (found 09:26 UTC on 2026-11-21 by round 8's R3, two severe issues on Jupiter's dive frame 2997). Each stop's `sky/patch.py` extends the approved image's sky past its border with a reflected copy, which keeps the border free of a step but shows the sky twice, mirrored, as soon as the camera turns in the dive, the rise or the hold's drift. The Sun's R4 saw the same seams scroll through its hold. `tools/assets/mirrorcheck.py` looks for it (correlation of each strip with the reflected strip beside it, on a high passed image; weak under motion blur, so it is a first look, not a gate). Every stop agent is told with its round result: outside the approved border fade into the film's shared star sky, inside change nothing. Mars has the same padding (weak hit on dive frame 2427 near x 1798); see open item 17.
- Phase 4, when an R3 critic faults something that is in the approved image (09:32 UTC on 2026-11-21). Round 12's R3 scored the chevron down Venus's cloud deck severe, as a longitude seam; the feather pattern is in the approved image and R1 scored the same frame 9. R3 does not see the approved image and its rubric is not changed. Decision: the approved image wins inside the hold view; the stop agent checks that the wrap has no real seam at that place from the dive and rise cameras and softens it only where the hold frame does not change. The issue is carried as open if a later critic raises it again. The same applies to the small comet like blob at the lower left of Mercury's hold.
- Phase 4, engine batch 10 is on main (09:51 UTC on 2026-11-21, merge of `builder/batch10` ffe0a2d plus two things, checked as `builder/batch10b` cf69c48; engine hash e96a669c7c94eb54, Mars hash unchanged 353338cc5c905af5). In it: (a) the belt refit of the Mars to Jupiter crossing (`agent/belt` 234fc04: `src/space/belt/belt.ts`, `motion.ts`, `belt.json`, the rocks placed in the flow of the new travel camera, near rock tilt and focus, the far set clear of Jupiter) and the belt agent's dust order request (`src/space/dust.ts`, one line in `src/core/engine.ts`); (b) Earth's engine request 2, accepted: `terrain.exitDipDeg?: number` in `src/director/moves.ts` (two uses, default 0, so every stop that does not set it is unchanged), which lets a terrain flight leave with the nose down so that the rise starts on the surface and not on the sky; (c) the harness agent's third part (`agent/harness` 393cdff): `tools/master.py` (`npm run master`, the plan, the split and the join of the film across workers), `tools/cues.ts` (`npm run cues`, writes and checks `cut/CUES.md`) and `tools/gauntlet.py` (`npm run gauntlet`, the QA list of Phase 6 with a result per item). Proof: build clean, 48 tests pass; on three render workers at the final tier Mars 2325, 2427, 2580 and 2726 are byte identical to the kept baselines (`out/work/baseline-73b8/`), and the three belt frames 2772, 2782 and 2796 are byte identical to the belt agent's own renders (`out/work/baseline-b10/`), so the merge changed nothing the agent had not shown. Mars's travel frame 2782 is new by design, as with batch 6; it is judged in Phase 5. Tried on main: `npm run master -- plan --tier review --workers 12` gives 284 jobs of at most 15 minutes, 63.7 machine hours, 5.4 hours on the busiest of twelve workers, so the review tier fits `sandbox.sandboxMasterMaxHours`; `npm run cues -- --check` fails as it must before Phase 6 (no `cut/CUES.md`, and `cut/SHOTS.md` is older than the plans, `npm run shots` runs at the Phase 4 gate); `npm run gauntlet` runs and counts 5 pass, 12 fail, 6 not run, 9 for a person or a critic, which is the true state today. What the belt agent itself calls weak, carried to Phase 5: no rock before frame 2754 or after 2802, lit faces light grey, far rocks carry no pan blur, the hero rock enters dark side first (2770 to 2773), one mask for all rocks (2784 to 2787), the web set not rendered again.
- Phase 4, stepped copies in fast motion (09:43 UTC on 2026-11-21). The motion critic of four first rounds faulted the same thing as major: bright detail in the fastest dive and rise frames breaks into three or four separate copies and is not smeared (Uranus 4126 to 4134 and 4420 to 4427, Neptune 4982 to 4986, Saturn 3596 to 3600 and 3835 to 3838; the Sun's rise frame 247 has stars as dashes in one part of the sky and points in another). Seen on Uranus 4126 at full size: the limb blurs smoothly, the white cloud puffs stand in discrete copies about 15 px apart. Review tier gives fast kinds 16 time samples over the 180 degree shutter, which should not leave steps that wide, so either a layer runs on a coarser clock than the sample's time or the count is too low for these speeds. It is an engine matter, not eight stop matters. The engine agent (`agent/engine-perf`) has the task: measure the screen motion over the shutter on those frames per layer, put any layer on the sample's time, and make the sample count follow the motion (step between samples at most a config value, a cap per tier, frames that need no more than today's count stay byte identical, the count in the frame record, the tools that estimate render time count the true samples). It goes onto main as a batch only with the usual byte proof on Mars. Stop agents are told not to fix this in their own folders.
- Phase 4, a lost critic spawner (09:38 UTC on 2026-11-21). Critics are started about ten at a time by a script that waits for running ones to answer. Ending my turn to wait for mail while that script ran interrupted it once (round 9: about half the critics were not started). Fix: the list of agents is read back, names already started are skipped, the script runs again; and while a spawner runs I wait on the script, not on mail. No critic was started twice and no answer was lost.
- Phase 4, Saturn's fallback is planned before it is needed (09:57 UTC on 2026-11-21). `kit/02-STOPS.md` names the change for Saturn's terrain view: a relief for the far and middle distance with true 3D ice near the lens, once true 3D has not matched the approved close image after `quality.plateauRounds` rounds. Round 13 gave R1 4 on that view with a failed jury. With three rounds in all the switch would fall on the last round with nothing left to correct it. Decision: round 2 stays true 3D as the kit orders, and the stop agent builds the relief fallback in parallel behind one flag; the round 2 scorecard decides which one round 3 runs on (rung logged then). For the hold the agent is advised to treat the rings as the globe is treated, the approved hero's ring pixels unprojected onto the ring plane and used at full strength inside the approved view (the ladder's second rung for an orbit view, applied to the rings).
- Phase 4, stepped copies: the cause is measured (09:59 UTC on 2026-11-21, engine agent, review tier, 1920 wide, every draw of every layer recorded). Every world layer is drawn at 16 distinct times in a fast frame; nothing is cached across samples and nothing runs on the frame number. The steps are too few samples for the speed: from first to last sample the globe moves 117 px in Neptune 4984 (7.8 px between neighbouring samples), 70 px in Uranus 4420 with its halo at 133 px (4.7 to 8.9 px), 39 to 60 px in Uranus 4126 to 4134, 8 to 20 px on Saturn 3598 and 3835 (its rings up to 52 px), against 3.8 px in Mars 2427. A step shows from about 2 px because each sample is drawn sharper than a pixel. The one layer on another clock is the engine sky, which is drawn with the middle of the shutter in every sample by design (`kit/04-LOOK.md` section 7, stars stay points; the Look lock records that the other way turned every star into a dash). Decisions: (1) the sky stays as it is; the engine agent's offer to put it on the sample's time is declined. (2) The sample count of a fast frame follows the motion: step at most 1.5 px, capped at 64 samples at the review tier and 128 at the final tier, draft unchanged, all three as config keys; this is the ladder's first rung for ghost copies, applied only to the frames that need it. (3) The Sun's dashed stars at 247 are its own sky card, a world mesh that moves 3.6 px across the samples beside engine stars that stand still: the Sun's agent takes the stars out of the card or puts it on the sky's clock through a small engine flag that is in work. (4) Saturn's lumpy ring blur is only partly sample count (steps of 0.3 to 3.5 px there); the engine agent looks at the ring picture and names the shader line for Saturn's agent.
- Phase 4, engine batch 11 is on main (11:02 UTC on 2026-11-21, merge of `builder/batch11` 7772d80, engine hash a9de32c960e5de06, Mars hash unchanged 353338cc5c905af5). In it: (a) the engine agent's second task (`agent/engine-perf` 5fa7485, its message starting DONE at 10:49 UTC, notes in `assets-src/shared/PERF.md` under "Second task"): the sample count of a frame follows its screen motion. `src/core/motion.ts` takes the camera and the set poses at the two ends of the shutter from the plans alone (no browser), follows the points that a grid of `motion.probeGrid` view rays hits on the body (and out to `motion.bodyReach` radii on a ringed stop) or on the ground of a terrain view (`motion.terrainGround` under the path, or the stop's new optional plan number `terrain.ground`), and asks for enough samples that neighbouring samples lie at most `motion.maxStepPx` (1.5 px at the master size) apart; never fewer than the tier's `samplesPerFrame` or `fastSamplesPerFrame`, never more than the tier's new `maxSamplesPerFrame` (draft 1, review 64, final 128). A frame that needs no more keeps its count and its sample pattern, so it is byte identical. The count is in every frame record (`extra.samples`), in `showcase.samples()` and in `npm run samples` (`tools/lib/samples.ts`), which `tools/pack.ts`, `tools/round.py`, `tools/budget.ts`, `tools/stress.ts` and `tools/master.py` now read for their job sizes. This is rung 1 of the ladder for "Fast motion breaks into ghost copies" (raise the fast sample count), applied per frame and not as one number per tier; `tiers.*.fastSamplesPerFrame` is unchanged. (b) A flag for a stop's own sky mesh: `mesh.userData.skyClock = true` draws that mesh in every sample where the middle of the shutter shows it (`src/core/held.ts`), as the engine sky is drawn, so its stars stay points (`kit/04-LOOK.md` section 7); default off, for the Sun's sky card. (c) Earth's engine request 3 (`agent/earth` 3d76305, `assets-src/earth/engine.patch`, one file, `src/director/moves.ts`), accepted: four optional plan keys, `dive.level` (where the dive's aim finishes levelling, default 0.97 as before), `terrain.entryDipDeg` (default 0), `rise.gentle` and `rise.from` (the height the climb starts from, default the dive's altitude); a stop that sets none of them is unchanged. Checked before the merge: build and 59 tests green here; at the final tier on 7772d80 from `render_01` and `render_02`: Mars 2325, 2427, 2580 and 2726 byte identical to the kept ones and the belt frames 2772, 2782, 2796 byte identical to batch 10's (`cmp`; sha256 prefixes 9895a050abe22d0c, 0a022282c24d2285, a7e1670abf3e4a24, 79352ec57fa5c00b, b0ee83126006c2ff, 9afa0648cb91ce3e, 09f326c5b85fe4e5), every record with the engine hash above and the Mars hash; at the review tier from `render_03`: Mars dive 2427 with 16 samples and the sha256 of the engine agent's proof (6cc683c40c226044), Neptune rise 4984 with 64 samples (175 s) and Uranus dive 4126 with 42 (58 s), looked at in native crops: the limb and the clouds smear without teeth. What 64 samples do not cure, seen in the same crop of Neptune 4984: the white cloud band stands in rectangular blocks, which is the stop's cloud map magnified, the Neptune agent's item from round 9. The cost, from `npm run samples -- --report`: the review tier film goes from 47,340 to 56,148 samples (+18.6 percent; 417 frames raised: 140 dive, 132 rise, 133 travel, 7 terrain, 5 retreat), the final tier from 189,360 to 190,349 (42 frames in mercury.dive, mercury.rise, earth.rise, jupiter.dive, jupiter.rise, uranus.rise, neptune.rise; no Mars frame). Mars at the review tier has raised frames in its dive (2441 to 2448), rise (2703 to 2720) and travel (2755 to 2766): its kept clip stays as it is and the master renders them with the new counts. `npm run master -- plan` on this tree: review tier 376 jobs, 76.5 machine hours, 6.4 hours on twelve workers; final tier 1,412 jobs, 263.1 machine hours, 22.0 hours on twelve workers, so the final tier fits `sandbox.sandboxMasterMaxHours` and more workers shorten it. Not changed: the engine sky stays on the mid shutter clock; no threshold, tier base count or judged frame.
- Phase 4, the machine checks between the rounds (10:02 UTC on 2026-11-21, main at a156ab3 with batch 10): `npm run gate -- --phase 4` gave G1, G2, G3, G4, G6, G7 (47 judged frames), G10, G11, G12 (1,729 files, 1.36 GB), G14 and G15 all PASS, with Mars the only finished stop. While the eight stop agents worked on round 2 the twelve workers were idle, so each rendered one contact sheet at the review tier from main e5c57ba: the eight travels, `ending.retreat`, `ending.lookback`, `ending.fade` (files in `out/work/sheets/p5look/`). Found: (1) the moves read as one flight and `earth.travel` is the model (dense stars and the Milky Way through 2195 to 2222, Mars growing from a point); `ending.lookback` and `ending.fade` are good; (2) in six of eight travels the middle frames are a near black sky with dust streaks only (sun 474 to 482, mercury 1055 to 1062, venus 1625 to 1639, mars 2765 to 2772, jupiter 3342 to 3355, saturn 3905 to 3919, uranus 4475), which the kit forbids as an empty black card; (3) the arriving stop's sky patch comes in as an island with a visible edge (venus.travel 1639 to 1645, mars.travel 2792 to 2799), and mercury.travel 1069 to 1082 shows two Milky Way bands in one frame; uranus.travel 4482 to 4489 shows a smeared band and then a very bright one; (4) in `ending.retreat` the sky is near black up to 5090 and full at 5101. Decision: the travel agent (idle since 07:31 UTC) takes this now on `agent/travel` as Phase 5 work started early: a living sky in every travel frame, no patch edge and no second band in flight, the retreat sky eased over its whole length, with no hold, dive, terrain or rise frame of any stop changing by one byte (proof by `cmp` at the draft tier on a list of frames, and my own final tier check on the Mars judged frames before the merge). The stop rounds are not touched by it: the first round scorecards (rounds 6 to 13) name no travel frame, the travel frames are judged in the whole film rounds of Phase 5. Two stop items seen on the sheets go to their agents with the next message: Venus's far side in blocks at 1618, thin green yellow strokes left of Saturn at 3362 and 3369.
- Phase 4, engine batch 12 is on main (11:12 UTC on 2026-11-21, `builder/batch12` f40b9c4, engine hash 020740ac07ff27f7, Mars hash unchanged). One change, the Uranus agent's engine request 1 (`assets-src/uranus/ENGINE-REQUESTS.md` on `agent/uranus` fd27cc1): in `Sky.render` the ray of the sky pass is built from the inverse projection and divided by the float32 sum of two of its terms; low over a planet (near plane 0.02 of the altitude, far plane 80000) the two terms are 250 to 1250 and on some frames cancel to exactly 0, so the whole sky pass (band, patches, Sun disc and corona) drops out for that one frame while the stars stay. The agent listed the frames hit in mercury, venus, earth, mars (2453, 2700, 2702), jupiter, uranus and neptune; it is the cause of "the Milky Way pops on between 4415 and 4416" in the Uranus scorecard. The fix: when that float32 sum is exactly 0 the two terms are set to 0 and 1, which gives the true ray; every other frame takes the old path with the old numbers. An order mistake of mine, logged as it happened: the batch went to main with the push of Status 22 at 11:12 (the status tool pushes the checked out branch, and I had not switched back to `main`) before the byte check had come back. The check came back at 11:14 and is clean: build and 59 tests pass, and Mars 2325, 2427, 2580 and 2726 at the final tier are byte identical to the kept baseline (`cmp` against `out/work/baseline-73b8/`, sha256 starting 9895a050abe22d0c, 0a022282c24d2285, a7e1670abf3e4a24, 79352ec57fa5c00b), so nothing had to be taken back. Looked at before and after at the review tier: Neptune 4724 had no Milky Way band above the limb and has it now; Mars 2453 and 2700 (cover full) are byte identical and Mars 2702 differs by at most 3 code values on 1,809 pixels, so the kept Mars clip shows no difference. Rule from now on: a status is pushed from `main` only; the tool is run after `git branch --show-current` says main.
- Phase 4, Sun, the rise cover, plateau rule (12:53 UTC on 2026-11-21). R4 marked the rise handover severe in round 10 ("pre- and post-cover frames do not belong to one continuous move") and again in round 15 ("the limb close-up cuts to a centered full disk through a full-frame whiteout"), so tuning stops and the technique changes. Ladder "A cover can be seen as a join": round 15 was rung 2 (a thicker cover, glare that fills the frame for seven frames, 212 to 218), and the critic faults exactly that. For round 3 the whiteout is withdrawn and rung 1 is done on the geometry and not only on the color: at the swap frames the relief view and the globe view show the limb arc at the same height with the same curvature, the sunspot region at the same place, the same exposure and the same direction of travel, joined by a short dissolve, with glare only as a partial veil bound to a footpoint. The limb has to be readable in every frame. The same reading is applied to every other stop whose cover turns into a featureless field.
- Phase 4, engine batch 13 is on main (13:30 UTC on 2026-11-21, `builder/batch13` 75c337d, engine hash 6728b99fa98fbd73, Mars hash unchanged). It is the travel agent's sky task (`agent/travel` 0ed917a, notes in `assets-src/travel/NOTES.md`), the answer to the first look at the travels (near black middles, sky patch islands, steps of the sky in the retreat). What changed: in every travel the lens opens as the bright bodies leave the frame (`src/space/travel/open.ts`: share of the frame the leaving body, the arriving body and the Sun disc cover), the glow of the shared sky goes to one flight level and stays there, the catalog stars go to an absolute level, and a flight star layer (600000 seeded points, drawn only when the lift is above 0) fills the field off the band; the arriving stop's sky patch comes in over the last quarter of the travel, after the camera is within 6 degrees of its final direction (`Shot.skyPatch`, with the old curve as the fallback); the retreat's sky opens the same way. Every number is in the new `travel.sky` section of `config/showcase.json`. Verified by the Builder, not taken from the agent's report: build and 59 tests pass on the branch; Mars 2325, 2427, 2580 and 2726 at the final tier, rendered on two workers, are byte identical to the kept baseline (`cmp` against `out/work/baseline-73b8/`); twelve draft stills outside Mars (90, 360, 870, 1440, 2010, 3150, 3720, 4290, 4860, 5145, 5235, 5360) rendered on one worker on ab7c0de and on 75c337d are byte identical pair by pair (`cmp`), so no hold, terrain, lookback or fade frame moves; all nine travel sheets were rendered at the review tier on the workers and looked at: every travel now has stars through its middle and no frame is an empty black card. Still weak, carried to Phase 5 (step 1, travels): the first ten frames or so of each travel keep the stop's near black sky while the leaving body fills the frame; the flight field is even, without clusters; an arriving patch's Milky Way band comes up out of a plain star field where the shared sky has no band under it (worst at mars.travel 2798 to 2816 and saturn.travel 3939 to 3946; the fix is a sky aim at the arriving stop that puts the shared band under the patch's band, as Venus did, and Jupiter has been asked for it in its round 3); uranus.travel 4482 to 4495, the band crosses the view in about 14 frames; mercury.travel 1089 to 1102, the band changes colour from blue grey to brown; venus.travel 1605 to 1625 passes the globe at close range. Stops are told to merge main before READY.
- Phase 4, Mercury, the covers, plateau rule (13:55 UTC on 2026-11-21). The motion critic's severe issue on Mercury's dive cover survived two rounds: round 7 "the white hold on sheet-03 (739-750) hides a cut, not one move: the sun sits on the right through the dive and on the upper left after it", round 18 "an unphysical beige-white flash blows out exposure over an airless world to mask a one-frame pop between frame 743 and 744" and "frames before and after the dive cover do not belong to one camera move"; the rise cover has the same pair. Tuning the veil stops. Ladder "A cover can be seen as a join": rung 1 was tried on colour and exposure only in round 2; round 3 does rung 1 on geometry (horizon line, direction of travel and the Sun's place on both sides of each swap, by turning and widening the lens through the dive so the Sun's disc leaves the frame and its glare arrives where the approved close image has it) together with rung 2 in the form the kit names for Mercury in `kit/02-STOPS.md` ("the Sun's glare sweeps across the lens during the fastest part of the dive"): a glare that travels across the frame with the camera's turn in place of a whole frame veil, with sky and limb readable in every frame. Rung 3 (a longer cover) is not used: a longer veil is what the critic faults.
- Phase 4, Saturn, true 3D stays (14:47 UTC on 2026-11-21). `kit/02-STOPS.md` says of Saturn's terrain view: "If true 3D has not matched the approved close image after `quality.plateauRounds` rounds, change to a relief for the far and middle distance and keep true 3D ice near the lens." Two rounds are run (13 and 20), so the point is reached and decided here. Finding after round 20: against the approved close image at 3720 the likeness critic names one major issue, the ice grain layer ("bright white speckles and small soft blobs are scattered over the ring bands"), and minors (limb glow width, the globe a few pixels low, 2 levels of exposure); it names nothing in the far or middle rings or in the globe behind them, G17 at 3720 passes (0.819), and I see the same at native size beside the approved image. The grain layer is the part the kit keeps in true 3D on either path, so the relief would replace the part that matches and keep the part that is faulted. The motion critic gives Saturn 8, the highest of any stop, where the stops with relief terrain views sit at 3 to 6, a terrain view that reads as a plate being zoomed among the reasons; and the recognition jury now passes (3 of 10 against 5 of 10, 0 of 10 in round 13). The three severe issues of round 20 are seams in the hold and the dive, which a relief of the terrain view would not touch. Decision: round 3, Saturn's last, stays true 3D; the grains are remade as the kit and the approved image show them (few, tiny, irregular, in the ring plane, near the lens only); the relief is not built. Plateau rule on the limb: a detached bright rim outside the lit limb was faulted in round 13 (hold) and in round 20 (dive 3567), so it is not tuned again; ladder "A globe looks plastic, flat or like a web demo", rung 2 (rebuild the atmosphere): the rim glow is drawn from the globe's own surface and the rings and the globe hide each other against one sphere.
- Phase 4, which build of a stop is kept at the round limit (14:57 UTC on 2026-11-21, written before any third round is scored). `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md`: "When a loop reaches its limit without passing: keep the best build of that stop". The builds of a stop are the ones judged in its rounds, each at its judged commit. They are ranked in this order: (1) machine checks and measures green before not green; (2) the distance from the pass bar, the sum over R1, R3 and R4 of how far each score is under `quality.passScore`, smaller first; (3) the higher R4, by Brandon's order to put the motion first; (4) fewer severe issues; (5) fewer major issues, a failed R2 counted as one, as the kit counts it; (6) the later round. The Builder looks at the judged frames and the sheets of the two best builds side by side before keeping one, and writes here if what is seen contradicts the ranking; the ranking decides unless a fault the critics did not see is found. When an earlier build wins, the stop's own folders (`src/stops/<id>/`, `public/assets/<id>/`, `assets-src/<id>/`) are taken from that round's judged commit onto the current engine, its judged stills are rendered again and looked at beside the round's own (the kit's "Freshness"), and the Stops table carries that round's scores. The open items of a stop are the ones of the kept build's round.
- Phase 4, fast frames, third engine task (15:05 UTC on 2026-11-21). Finding: Earth frame 1870 moves 18.9 px during the shutter and is drawn with 16 samples, a step near 1.2 px, yet it reads as a comb of sharp dashes. Cause: each sample is drawn with the texture detail of a still frame, so thin bright lines stay one pixel sharp in every sample and the sum is a row of lines. Frames at the sample cap (Earth 1875 to 1879) step 2 to 4 px, and a translucent cover (Uranus 4159 to 4161) lowers the sample count although the picture still shows through. Ladder "Fast motion breaks into ghost copies": rung 1 again (caps measured and raised where the master still fits) together with a texture detail level that follows the motion (soft from 10 px, full at 18 px of shutter motion, so every Mars proof frame stays byte identical: the fastest is 9.09 px) and a plan number per stop for how much its cover hides (default as today). Rung 2 (blur along the motion inside a sample) is held back: it needs a velocity pass and costs every frame. Given to engine_profiler on agent/engine-perf; merged only after the Mars byte proof.

All eight stop agents were told not to send READY for round 3 before the batch is on main and merged, and to use the time on the motion items of their results. Cutoff: if the batch is not on main by 16:45 UTC, READY is sent without it. No limit, rubric or judged frame changes. Verified by the Builder: build and 62 tests pass on the merge; Mars 2325, 2427, 2580 and 2726 at the final tier, rendered on two workers on cf8154f, are byte identical to the baseline (sha256 9895a050, 0a022282, a7e1670a, 79352ec5); their sample counts are unchanged (16, 64, 16, 64). Mars travel frames 2772, 2782 and 2796 change on purpose (a star field where the frame was near black). Earth 1870 and 1872 at review tier on cf8154f (32 and 60 samples, were 16 and 24) are one smooth blur with no comb. With the agent's 1.0 to 2.4 s per sample that is 58 to 138 machine hours for the final master, 5 to 12 hours on twelve workers, inside `sandbox.sandboxMasterMaxHours`. The slowest frame measured is Earth 1877 at final: 162 samples in 755 s, memory flat at 3.0 GB; a 256 sample frame of that dive takes about 20 minutes. Fast frames of finished Mars get more samples at both tiers (2445 at final: 97, was 64); by the freshness rule its judged frames were compared, not its rounds run again.

- Phase 4, a worker returned black frames with exit code 0, and the harness now refuses them (17:40 UTC on 2026-11-21). Finding: the round 22 rise sheet from one worker had its last frames (4990 to 5024) pure black. Its log held ten lines `THREE.WebGLState: TypeError: Failed to execute 'texSubImage2D' ... Overload resolution failed`. Cause: the page lost its drawing context in the middle of the job (software drawing under memory pressure), the browser restored it, and the engine could not upload its textures again because `src/core/sets.ts` frees each decoded image after the first upload; every frame after that is black and the tool still wrote it. The Builder's own sheet of the same segment on the same commit has no such line and twelve good frames, so the frames themselves are sound. Decision: (1) capture side, now: `tools/lib/browser.ts` counts lost contexts and reads the page's console for the upload and shader errors, `renderPng` throws on either, and stills and sheets render the frame again on a fresh page (up to 3 tries); the film render stops with exit code 3 and is resumed by running the same command again. Proved by forcing a context loss in a test: the old symptom appeared (18 upload errors), the guard threw, the fresh page drew the frame. `npx tsc --noEmit` and 62 tests pass, the engine hash is unchanged (f854c8146f64c7e4), so open rounds and records stay valid. (2) Engine side, with batch 16: the app frees and loads its sets again when the context is restored, so the live app does not go black either. (3) The broken sheet is not shown to critics; the segment is rendered again on two workers and the Builder looks at every tile before the pack. Every sheet and every clip is checked for black frames before it is used.

In `aimSky()` (`src/director/film.ts`) a stop whose predecessor has no cover on its rise follows the predecessor's sky turn, and Saturn in true 3D has no cover, so the sky of the Uranus hold, dive, terrain view and rise turned with the merge (the Uranus agent measured its own aim at [-92.27, -170.85, 64.61] and a dark, sparse sky around its hold, with the band about 50 degrees off). - Phase 4, why the dark sky of a later stop moves by one level when an earlier stop is merged (19:00 UTC on 2026-11-21, found by the Saturn agent on the Builder's question, read only). Drafts of Saturn's frames differed between main 1259820 and main 530168f in about 0.5 percent of the sky pixels by one level (3465: 5,105 px; 3866: 9,833 px, a few up to 16 levels where the shared sky shows instead of the hold patch), with the globe untouched. Cause: `place()` builds each stop's world frame from the leaving pose of the stop before it, so Jupiter's new rise turned Saturn's frame by 158 degrees; the sky turn is then rounded to seven decimals (`src/director/film.ts`, `round`, used in `aimSky`), which leaves the camera to sky rotation 4.4e-6 degrees off, about 1.5e-4 px at this lens, enough to flip the rounding of a dense star field here and there. Mars is held by the world pin (decision of 06:10 UTC) and its draft hash of 2325 is unchanged after the Sun, Jupiter and Saturn merges (fd128b1a999f76fa). Decided: nothing is changed now. A stop is judged on the commit of its own round, the frozen build renders every judged still again in Phase 6, and the difference is far under anything a critic or a viewer can see. Neptune's clip of Phase 4 was rendered on 530168f and is therefore not byte equal to a render after Saturn's merge; the nine clips are made again from the master in Phase 6.

When its last span came back main had Saturn's third round build merged, and `npm run master -- join` refused the join because it compared the hashes of all nine stops with the plan's. The film tool and `npm run keep` call a chunk or a clip current when the engine hash and the hashes of the stops its frames read are the plan's (for a stop's four segments: that stop), and `place` had judged every span by that same rule. `tools/master.py` join now uses it too and names the stops that changed when it refuses. What this leaves, as written at 19:00 UTC: the frames of a stop after Mars can differ by one level in about half a percent of the dark sky pixels from a render on today's main; the clip is the Phase 4 review clip and the nine clips are cut again from the frozen build's master in Phase 6.

- Phase 5 step 4, the sky grade: one band look for the film (decided 00:55 UTC on 2026-11-22). `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 5 asks for one sky "graded to match the approved images", and R5 asks "one grade, one lens, one sky". Measured by eye on the montage of the 18 approved images: the band is warm grey brown to gold with dark dust in seven of them (strongest at Uranus, Jupiter and Venus), cool grey blue only at Mars, and Saturn's sky is plain. The shared band was graded against Mars in Phase 3, which is why it is blue violet with olive haze, and the travel agent's fourth task graded the flight band gold and tied that grade to the flight lift, so the colour follows the exposure. Decision: (a) colour and exposure are separated: the flight lift changes brightness only; (b) the band's look becomes a weight per stop between today's shared look (0) and the warm flight grade (1), held for every segment of the stop, and in a travel it moves from the leaving stop's weight to the arriving stop's together with the arriving sky patch, slowly and only once; the ending keeps Neptune's weight, so the retreat and the lookback show one band; (c) the engine change lands with every weight at 0, so every stop frame stays the same bytes and the proof stays a byte proof; (d) the weights are then set to 1 for every stop but Mars in one commit of their own (Mars stays at 0: it is approved and its frames stay the same bytes), and every finished stop's judged stills are rendered again and looked at beside the kept ones as Freshness asks, the rise frames too, since that is where the shared band shows (Saturn 3866, Uranus 4436, Earth 2156). If a stop looks worse with the warm band its weight goes back to 0 and the travels on both sides carry the change. (e) The arriving sky patch comes in over at least twenty frames and the band is not switched off where Saturn's plain sky comes in: the patch fades in while the lift closes. This is the travel agent's sixth task, with the judged travel frames (the next body as a point of light and the band or a dense field in frame at 1642, 3922 and 4492).

- Phase 5 step 7, the render budget measured again (02:25 to 02:50 UTC on 2026-11-22). The Builder's VM runs no render, so `npm run budget -- --stop mars` and `--stop earth` ran on a worker from main 3d485819 (job p5c-budget on render_03, back 02:43 UTC), and the cost per sample of every segment was taken from the records of the 48 judged frames rendered at the final tier on the same build (the 37 of the freshness run b17b and the eleven of p5c-tr-a and p5c-tr-b), multiplied by the director's sample count of every frame (`work/p5c/samples.json`: draft 5,400, review 88,698, final 209,488). Result, in the new block under \"Render budget\": the mean is 2.10 s per sample in master on four cores, the whole film at the final tier is 122 machine hours as an upper number (139 with set loads and job starts by `npm run master -- plan --workers 20 --fit --sample-seconds 2.10`: 661 jobs of at most 15 minutes, 7.0 hours on the busiest of twenty workers), at the review tier 49 machine hours. Decisions from it: (a) the master of Phase 6 step 5 is rendered at the final tier, the highest tier, since it fits `sandbox.sandboxMasterMaxHours` about three times over; (b) the preview film of Phase 5 step 9 is rendered at the review tier in the preview format on the workers (about 19 machine hours, one hour on twenty), not at the draft tier as in Phase 2, so the film on the review page shows the motion blur the critics judge; (c) the uhd format at the final tier would be about 340 machine hours, 17 hours on twenty workers, noted for Phase 6 and not planned, because `formats.master` is the deliverable.

- Phase 5, the cause of frames that do not repeat (03:10 UTC on 2026-11-22). The Saturn agent found it on its own stop (`agent/saturn` 7dc2542, notes in `assets-src/saturn/NOTES.md` under "Item 8"): the harness GPU shades 2 x 2 pixel quads; a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing to variables, so a local first assigned after the discard holds stale memory, and `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and `texture()` without an explicit level read it for the live neighbours of the quad. Its haze shell began with a discard and later took `dFdx` inside the ring shadow. After its fix (no discard before the last statement, no cross pixel operation on a value first assigned in a per pixel branch) 3866, 3594, 3567 and 3840 gave one hash each in 11 renders over 3 fresh sessions, where 3866 gave 6 hashes in 12 renders before. What the Builder did with it: the engine agent's task 5 became an audit of every fragment shader for the pattern, shared code first, with a fix on `agent/engine-batch19` and a list per stop folder (MAIL 62); every stop agent got the rule for its own shaders with its next commit (MAIL 63, with the news that batch 18 is on main). G3 as the kit defines it (the first hold, dive, travel and terrain segment, fresh loads and reversed order) is green on main and does not sample the frames in question, so no machine check is red; the fix reaches main with Saturn's polish in Phase 6 and with batch 19 for shared code. First answers: Neptune found three early discards with no derivative after them and moves them; Uranus moved its discards to the last statement in 19b03e1, its four finals keep their bytes.
- Phase 5, the open sky in flight, first PART of the travel agent's task 7 (03:35 UTC on 2026-11-22, `agent/travel` fd4fad6, which holds main 3d48581). A second point set of 450,000 points on the one sky (`travel.sky.flightStars.deep`, level = flight lift x `deep.level`, not drawn at lift 0, fixed positions, no time term) and a dust glow as a second channel of the flight map (`travel.sky.flightStars.veil`). Its numbers at the final tier, frame scaled to 456 px, boxes of 152 by 128, mean level and pixels above 24: 1625 from 5 to 10 and 53 to 158 before to 10 to 16 and 492 to 1220; 3912 from 4 to 11 and 68 to 178 to 8 to 17 and 333 to 1277; nine draft hold, dive and terrain frames byte equal to a tree built from 3d48581. The Builder looked at 1625, 3912, 482 and 2772 at 456 and 1625 at 1920: none reads as an empty card any more, the points have colour and stay points, but the field is so even that at 456 it reads as a fine grain over the frame and not as a sky (the agent says the same). Sent back with targets (MAIL 64): a long tail in brightness with a few dozen clearly brighter stars per frame, glow and density uneven at a quarter to a half of the frame, the low corner boxes up, proof that the new set does not smear where the camera turns, the venus.travel sheet to see the points come up with the lift, and a merge of today's main before the next proof. DONE asked for 04:05 UTC. Round 30 still waits for it.

- Phase 5, engine batch 19 DONE (03:51 UTC on 2026-11-22, `agent/engine-batch19` 03dcf90, which held main 0622510). It confirms the Saturn agent's cause by its own trace (the call stream of two renders is equal, the first draw that differs is Saturn's haze shell) and audits every fragment shader for the pattern in three classes: R (a derivative or a `texture()` without a level on a value first assigned after a per pixel `discard` or inside a per pixel branch: stale, must be fixed), F (the value is assigned before, so every lane holds it: defined on this GPU, fixed so it stays defined), D (diagnostic maps only). Shared code: two fixes (d4a7ab6 in the relief, 206ada0 in `src/engine/texel.ts` as `texelPlan` and in the sky band loop), no frame changed (49 repeatable draft frames byte equal to main a67c9c34, Mars's four finals equal), 79 tests. New tools: `tools/repeat.ts` (`npm run repeat -- --frames a,b --sessions N --runs N [--tier draft] [--stop ID]`, renders the same frames in fresh sessions and compares hashes) and `python3 tools/shaderaudit.py` (the static side). Stop code is not the engine agent's to change: its lists went word for word to Mercury, Jupiter, Neptune, Venus and Saturn as MAIL 65 (Mercury deck.ts and io.ts F; Jupiter sunplate.ts and lens.ts R in principle, skyplate.ts F; Neptune closeup.ts D; Saturn rings.ts F, its branch cures everything real; Venus globe.ts F, its cures in 47d7c6b confirmed with one hash each for 1300 and 1307 in 6 renders over 2 sessions). Main's Saturn frames stay unstable by a few pixels until the Saturn branch lands in Phase 6 (the cure moves 3 to 8 pixels by 1 to 2 levels on 3465, 3567, 3594, 3840 and 36 pixels by up to 26 levels on 3866); G3 does not sample those frames and is green. MAIL 66 to the engine agent: merge main, try the merge with `agent/travel` in a scratch branch, run the audit on it and read the two new sky shaders, then `repeat` on 482, 495, 1625, 2772, 3912, 4502, 5085, 5235 with 3 sessions and 2 runs when `builder/b19-pre` is pushed.

- Phase 5, the open sky dither (04:00 UTC on 2026-11-22). The engine agent read the travel agent's two new shaders on a scratch merge: both clean for the discard pattern, the eight travel frames gave one hash each over two fresh sessions. Its one finding: the dust glow's dither was `fract(sin(dot(gl_FragCoord.xy, ...)) * 43758.5453)`, whose value hangs on the last bit of the GPU's `sin`, the same on one machine every time but not promised across machines, and the master is rendered on twenty workers whose frames the Builder compares by hash. MAIL 67 asked the travel agent for an integer hash of the pixel instead (multiply, shift, xor on `uint`); it is on `agent/travel` as 4681511 (04:03). The Builder read the whole diff of the branch (`src/space/sky.ts`, `src/space/travel/field.ts`, the travel block of the config): the second point set and the glow are built on the CPU from seeded streams in a fixed order, the glow is read with an explicit level, the dither is 0.12 of the glow (under half a level on screen, fixed to the pixel, no frame number), and the new view list only thins what reaches the vertex shader (a cone round the frame's diagonal with a margin, in index order, so the first flight set draws the same pixels as before).
- Phase 5 steps 1 and 4, the open sky in flight, the travel agent's DONE for task 7 (04:12 UTC on 2026-11-22, `agent/travel` 94b008d and its head 4681511a with the integer dither of MAIL 67). The second pass: a long tail brightness law, 760,000 points in `travel.sky.flightStars.deep` with groups and a soft halo on the brightest, a dust glow with clouds and lanes, `deep.lens` and `veil.lens` at 1 so the vignette is not applied twice to the new layers, and a view list (`Sky.viewStars`) that sends only the points inside a cone round the frame to the vertex shader for both flight sets, which makes the final tier frames 2 to 4 percent faster than before the task. Its numbers in 152 by 128 boxes of the frame scaled to 456 px (mean level, pixels above 24): 1625 10.4 to 17.0 and 463 to 1260; 3912 9.5 to 15.5 and 512 to 1016; 482 9.1 to 24.2 and 335 to 7522; 2772 7.4 to 13.8 and 350 to 1464, against 4 to 7 and 50 to 120 on batch 18. The Builder looked at 1625, 3912, 482 and 2772 at 456 px and at 1920 (1625 also enlarged): single stars and small groups, warm, blue and a few rose points, soft cores on the bright ones, the dust motes as short faint streaks in front. It reads as a star field and no longer as an empty card or a grain; it is still fairly even across the frame and the glow regions are subtle. Verdict: good enough to go to the critics; it is rung 1 of the ladder \"The sky looks fake\" in `kit/04-LOOK.md` section 9 (more stars as points, with more variety in brightness and color), applied before a critic had to name it.

## Open items

One entry per issue left open by a round limit: the stop, the frame, the critic and its words, what was tried, what to try next.

1. Whole film, frames 615 to 5006, R4 blockout round 2, severe: "every stop from Mercury through Neptune repeats the exact same 570-frame pacing cycle and dead-center bullseye hold framing with an on-axis zoom". Tried: a different arrival for each stop (side, swing, turn angle, turn length, and an off-center start that drifts to the hero framing). Not changed: the hold framing, which is the approved hero image, and the segment times, which are Brandon's in `config/showcase.json`. Next: per stop in Phases 3 and 4, vary what moves inside the hold (orbit direction and amount, moon paths, how far the push goes) and give each dive and rise its own line. If Brandon wants uneven stop lengths, that is a config change through an addendum.
2. Ending, frames 5145 to 5362, R4 blockout round 2, major: "camera is nearly frozen for over 200 frames on a mostly black frame". Tried after the round: continuous pull back, a 3 degree circle round Neptune and a 4.5 degree pan on one ease to the last frame. Next: judged by R4 and R5 in Phase 5 with the finished Neptune and sky.
3. `mars.travel`, frames 2745 to 2819, both blockout rounds, major: "Mars whips off-screen in 6 frames", "Jupiter scales straight out of the dead center". Tried: earlier reveal, a longer ease, a swing, an off-center start, a longer turn. Next: judge it with motion blur at the review tier in Phase 5. If the turn still reads as a whip, start it inside `mars.rise`.
4. `sun.terrain`, frames 0 to 179, both blockout rounds, major: "almost completely static". Tried: a lateral creep and a longer push on the flat stand-in. Next: the Sun's relief in Phase 4, with loops as their own layers in front of the surface, and a glide that shows it.
5. `mars.dive`, frames 2400 to 2489, R4 blockout round 2, major: "zooms flatly on-axis into Mars (pushing Phobos awkwardly out the bottom border), snaps pitch abruptly into the canyon horizon at 2465 ... then stalls". Next: rebuilt in Phase 3: arc past Phobos, carry the horizon angle and one canyon bend through the dust cover, keep gliding into the terrain move.
6. mars, `mars.rise`, frames 2695 to 2706, R4 round 5, major: "The nearly level terrain horizon disappears into a stationary-looking horizontal haze band. As that band clears, a curved orbital limb and a different-looking star field register within two frames, so the pullback reads as a concealed cut rather than the same camera move." Tried: the cover carries the ground color, the exposure and the horizon line across the change of view (before round 3); stars fade in as the dust clears (after round 3); the dust thins over seven frames on the orbit side (`coverEdgeRise`, after round 4); the dust is held inside the planet's air and the climb has no roll (round 5). Next: dust that streams with the climb, as `coverRush` does in the dive, so the band is not level and still; a thinning of ten frames or more on the orbit side so the limb's curve grows in view; the canyon sky patch faded out across the pullback, not switched off under the cover, so the stars after the cover are the ones before it.
7. mars, `mars.dive`, frames 2456 to 2460, R4 round 5, major: "The radial streaks fade over an already recognizable canyon image, whose foreground cliffs and horizon resolve almost in place. The dive appears to stop under a blur dissolve rather than ease into the terrain glide." Tried: speed, heading, pitch and roll at the cover center derived from the canyon entry (Phase 3 swoop); the plunge moved inside a cover that holds and streams (after round 4); `entryHeight` 0.4 with `entryBack` 0.5, the largest entry offset that stays clean (0.65 with 0.48 and larger show a warped sliver at the lower left in 2458 to 2461). Seen by the Builder in the review clip: the canyon is nearly still from 2458 to 2463. Next: carry the plunge's speed through the first second of the canyon view, which needs relief painted further behind the entry point than the aprons reach; then let the streaks taper over more frames while the foreground still moves.
8. mars, `mars.hold`, frame 2325, R1 round 5, major, the sunlit limb: "the planet disc terminates in a hard, stair-stepped aliased circular cutout against the outer glow instead of blending through a soft translucent blue-white atmospheric veil over the surface edge, and near the bottom-left frame edge the blue outer atmospheric glow disappears completely". Tried: the limb glow fitted to the approved hero at five places and ten distances (after round 3), a ground haze fitted to the hero's limb ring, a glint layer (after round 3), the halo refitted against the hero's own sky (round 4), a grazing haze with narrow cool and warm cores and an oblique taper on detail near the limb (`agent/mars-limb`, after round 4). The glow outside the disc measures within a few code values of the hero; the disc's own edge is still the sphere's edge. Next: one haze layer drawn across the limb, over the last pixels of the disc and the first pixels of sky, so the edge is made by air and not by geometry; extend the lit range of the shell down the lower left limb.
9. mars, `mars.hold`, frame 2325, R1 round 5, major, the night limb: "the night-side limb is a razor-sharp dark geometric cutout slicing abruptly into the starfield instead of a soft faint-blue atmospheric rim falloff, the subtle blue cloud patches near the lower-right limb are lost to flat dark brown-black, and a stray blurry blue-white spot appears in space just outside the upper-right limb". 16. Export proof, last frame, found 08:34 UTC on 2026-11-21 at the start of rounds 6 to 10. The proof of Phase 2 was made with the stand-in Sun, so G6, which compares the proof's first frame with a fresh still, went red (13.8 dB) as soon as the real Sun was on main: the gate compared two different films. The proof was made again on this build (`npm run proof`): frame 0, the key frame, 30.53 dB against its still, so G6 is green again; frame 29 is 25.38 dB, under the proof tool's own 30. Side by side the two frames show the same picture, the export one softer: the page's H.264 encoder at `previewFilm.bitrateMbps` (2) cannot hold the Sun's granulation in a predicted frame. No bar is changed. `tools/round.py` now makes the proof again before the gate whenever the engine or the stop that owns frame 0 has changed, so G6 always compares like with like. To settle before the Phase 6 proof (G9 and gauntlet item 15 ask that the proof exists from the final build and that `ffprobe` accepts it; neither asks for the last frame's PSNR, but the tool reports it as a failure and that is not hidden): either the Sun's first second holds 30 dB once its look is final, or the engine's export at the preview size gets the bitrate it needs from a config number, taken with an engine batch and a byte check, never by lowering 30.
