28. venus, `venus.rise`, frames 1555 to 1566 (cover sheet), R4 round 23, two majors: "starfield and sun glow collapse to black in two frames, stay pure black through 1559-1561, then pop back brighter than the terrain sky" and "terrain clouds dissolve into an untrackable wash and the disc that emerges is a different surface, so the cover is not one move" Seen by the Builder on the dive cover sheet as well: the sky is black from 1311 to 1319 and the Sun comes up as a red disc. Tried. Round 2: the hold sky patch carried into the shared sky, the covers given a haze band (judged in round 16 as a straight band with a second, curved limb under it). Round 3: the veil's edge is the limb in both views (`coverShell` in the orbit view, `coverSea` on the relief's own sphere in the terrain view), uniform cover colours so no straight band is left, `veil.peak 0.94` with billows of its own (`coverRush { speed 5, reach 1.0, billow [3.5, 3.5, 1.0], streak [9, 6, 1.6] }`), `terrain.exitDipDeg 4.65` puts the terrain horizon of 1559 (0.135 of the frame height) on the orbit limb of 1560 (0.140), `terrain.exitPitchDeg 2.2` takes the Sun out of the orbit view at 1560, `coverHides 0.7` gives the swap frames 92 to 128 samples, and the globe's texture footprint grows with the motion in the shutter so the bright clumps do not show as hard cards. The terrain air dome is dimmed and reddened under the veil (`relief.update(..., sky)`), which is what turns the sky black and the Sun red. Without the veil the two views agree in the place and tilt of the horizon and not in content (dark cumulus and the Sun on the terrain side, the tan globe with white strokes on the orbit side), so the veil has to hide the swap frames. Not possible inside the stop's folder: a cover longer than the 16 frame window (the amount is above 0 on 14 frames in the rise and 17 in the dive, peak 0.94). Next, Phase 6: the stars and the Sun's glare kept at their own exposure through the veil; the terrain clouds carried out as streaks along the pull back and the first orbit frames graded toward the terrain picture; a longer window through the per stop cover window of the engine (engine agent, task 5).

## Running jobs

One entry per long job while it runs: the command, the frame range, the log file, the VM, when it started.

Phase 4 supporting agents, started 2026-11-21 about 02:42 UTC, each on its own VM. ## Rounds

One line per round, written when the round starts: number, phase, stop. Scores and what changes next are added when it ends.

- Round 1, Phase 2, blockout, whole film. R4 blockout 7, three major, two minor. Next: each stop arrives on its own curve, the approach starts earlier and eases longer, the lookback is reframed with a slow pan. `scorecards/round-001/`.
- Round 2, Phase 2, blockout, whole film. R4 blockout 5, one severe, four major, two minor. Limit reached. After the round: arrivals start off center, the lookback never stops moving, the turn away from Mars is longer. Open items 1 to 5. `scorecards/round-002/`.
- Round 3, Phase 3, mars, look lock round 1 of 5. Started 2026-11-20 UTC on main at 93ef0b0, judged on 696e228. G5, G13, G17 green. R1 6, R2 pass, R3 4, R4 6, R8 pass. Three severe (smear on both judged motion frames, blocky polar cap), majors on the limb glow, the Milky Way and stars, star dashes, a terrain blob, flat and lifeless terrain flight. Next: retime dive and rise so the speed sits in the cover, wide soft limb haze, sky band and star counts, sky drawn with the mid-shutter lens, relief depth fix, a real terrain glide with drifting dust. `scorecards/round-003/`.
- Round 4, Phase 3, mars, look lock round 2 of 5. Started 2026-11-20 UTC on main at 24c2334. Machine checks green before the pack (G1, G2 with 22 tests, G3, G4, G6, G7, G10, G11, G12, G14, G15). Judged on 1d1a55c after the sky was changed inside the round. G5, G13, G17 green. R1 8, R2 pass, R3 5 (parts 5 and 7), R4 6, R8 pass. No severe issue. Majors: both dust covers paint over black space outside the limb and the canyon's direction does not carry across them, a 75 degree roll after the rise cover (R4); the lit limb is harder and speckled with stars too close to the rim (R1); airbrushed surface near the left limb, soft Phobos, a soft polar cap in the rise, hook strokes on the dark side at 2427, and two shapes in the canyon that the approved hero also has (R3). Next: the motion first, by Brandon's change of plan: the dive retimed with the plunge inside a cover that holds, a terrain glide with real forward travel, dust kept inside the planet's air on the orbit side, one heading through both covers. `scorecards/round-004/`.
- Round 5, Phase 3, mars, look lock round 3 of 5. Started 2026-11-20 UTC on main at 0ee8c76. The last round before the Mars review, by Brandon's change of plan. In it: the dive retimed with the plunge under a cover that holds, a terrain glide of 1.65 camera heights over extended relief, dust held inside the planet's air on the orbit side, a climb out with no roll, and the limb, cap and sky patch fixes made after round 4. Machine checks green before the pack (G1, G2 with 22 tests, G3, G4, G6, G7, G10, G11, G12, G14, G15). G5, G13, G17 green. R1 7, R2 pass, R3 5 (parts 7 and 5), R4 6, R8 pass. No severe issue. Majors: the rise cover reads as a concealed cut and the canyon resolves almost in place as the dive cover thins (R4); the lit limb is a stepped cutout, the night limb is razor sharp, speckle and stepped rings near the limb and on the cap (R1); the storm spiral reads as painted, the pullback shows a smeared surface, a blown-out cap and no soft terminator, and the canyon view has muddy shadows and a flat hot horizon band (R3). The canyon view drew no motion issue. No fix follows: by Brandon's change of plan the build goes to the Mars review with open items 6 to 15. `scorecards/round-005/`.
- Round 6, Phase 4, uranus, round 1 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 08:19 UTC. Judged on main at 47a7600. Measures green. R1 7, R2 pass, R3 5, R4 4, R8 pass. 2 severe, 13 major. Next: Worst first. 1. The dive cover (severe), 4150 to 4175: the two sides are not one move. A level pale teal limb with an upright Milky Way column and no Sun at 4150 becomes a deep cobalt horizon tilted about 20 degrees with a bright Sun, double rings overhead and a sudden drop in camera speed by 4175. Bring pose, roll, horizon angle, Sun place, ring geometry, colour, exposure and speed into line on both sides. 2. The rise cover (severe), 4405 to 4416: a dark cobalt horizon with the Sun at the right limb and ring arcs overhead swaps behind the cloud wipe to a bright front lit mint globe with no Sun and one side ring, and the Milky Way pops on in one frame between 4415 and 4416. Match light, Sun, ring orientation, exposure and curvature, and keep the sky drawn all through the cover. 3. The cover itself, 4159 to 4170: the streaked veil stands nearly still for seven frames where it should carry the dive, breaks into upright dark and bright bands over the rings at 4166 to 4168, and the dust lane inside the rings pops on between 4169 and 4170. 4. The terrain picture's edge, 4175 to 4216: its bottom right edge and corner come into frame with clamped, stretched border streaks. 5. Stepped copies in fast motion: cloud puffs and thin ring edges in four separate copies at 4126 to 4134 and three to four at 4420 to 4427, where a smooth blur belongs (the engine side is taken up by the Builder for all stops). 6. Match to approved: ring strands and Milky Way dust softer than approved in the hold, with about three quarters of the highlight detail and faint ring gaps merged; ring lanes and limb filaments softer in the terrain view, worst where the rings cross the glare; the limb beside the Sun softer and brighter. 7. Craft at native size: cloud detail smeared into parallel streaks (dive 4137, terrain 4290 near field); the night side shadow as a separate crescent with its own hard inner edge (rise 4436); storms pasted on; rings in the rise as faint wobbly threads that do not follow one ellipse and show only behind the planet; a moon with a hard dark edge like two layers. 8. Minor: mirror repeated stars along the top and right sky margins of the terrain view; one pixel stars flickering and the thin inner ring beading in the hold; a squashed moon and a flat white one; a ring arc drawn too evenly; banding in the darkest corner. `scorecards/round-006/`.
- Round 7, Phase 4, mercury, round 1 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 08:19 UTC. Judged on main at 47a7600. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 5, R4 5, R8 pass. 1 severe, 11 major. Next: Worst first. 1. The dive cover (severe): the white hold 739 to 750 hides a cut, not one move. The Sun is on the right through the dive and on the upper left after it, a surface impact becomes a settled low skim, and exposure drops from a white veil at 755 to a hard Sun and black sky by 763. Keep one approach line through the cover so Sun side, horizon and height agree on the far side, and let the veil fall off over more frames. 2. Dive speed, 722 to 730: scale grows by about 0.03 per 8 frames up to 722 and by about 0.23 in the next 8; spread the acceleration so speed and field of view build without a step. 3. The rise: the departure lunges at 960 to 973 into a solid white hold (985 to 995) that erases the horizon, and the globe after it has another scale and exposure; ease the pullback and keep a horizon and the exposure readable through the cover. 4. The lens flare: against the approved image the diagonal amber streak and the broad green blue ghosts across the disc are missing, and what is there instead, a straight level streak of even width from the limb into the corona (with a reddish arc in the dive), reads as a pasted layer. Build the flare as approved shows it, soft and uneven, and tie it to the Sun's place in frame so it moves in the dive. 5. The globe in the rise frame 1016: craters and regolith smeared into parallel diagonal streaks at the lower left and along the western terminator, and the lit surface soft and upscaled at native size. 6. The terrain view 870: brighter stars sit in dark punched out rings and there are black pinholes in the star field; hundreds of one to three pixel coloured sparkles on the rock and bright dots with dark rings inside crater shadows. 7. The Sun disc: flat cream with airbrushed mottling; keep the approved exposure but give the disc a graded falloff. 8. Minor: limb stair steps and a doubled rim, a teal fringe on the unlit limb in the dive, column banding in the near black sky at the bottom edge of the hold, stamped starburst glints at the ray craters, wire like spikes at the double ray feature, near rock blurred with colour fringing, a one to two pixel amber fringe along the horizon. `scorecards/round-007/`.
- Round 8, Phase 4, jupiter, round 1 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 08:19 UTC. Judged on main at 47a7600. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 4, R4 6, R8 pass. 2 severe, 7 major. Next: Worst first. 1. The sky: the hold sky patch is padded with a mirror image, so the dive frame shows the sky reflected across the approved image's border (both severe issues; the same cause is in every stop and is fixed in each stop's sky folder; check tool tools/assets/mirrorcheck.py). 2. Both covers: carry the moving haze into the terrain view for several more frames and match rim sharpness and star visibility on both sides (dive 3028 to 3029); in the rise align the limb on both sides and bring rim brightness and stars back over ten frames or more (3269 to 3274). 3. The limb: one haze layer across the limb on the night side and on the lower left lit side, in place of the hard circular edge and its stair steps. 4. Io: a finer color map and a feathered edge with no dark fringe; its shadow as a closed ellipse with a penumbra. 5. The band and the stars in the dive frame 2997: coarse texels and square stars, so check the patch's scale there. 6. Terrain view (minor): more sideways travel so the near ridges cross faster than the horizon, and band flow round the storm that can be followed. R1's minors: the lower two thirds of the terrain view one to two levels darker and less warm than approved, the night limb a few pixels inward, stars a little busier. `scorecards/round-008/`.
- Round 9, Phase 4, neptune, round 1 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 08:19 UTC. Judged on main at 47a7600. Measures green. R1 6, R2 pass, R3 5, R4 5, R8 pass. 1 severe, 11 major. Next: Worst first. 1. Against round 16: the judged hold and terrain frames are the same pictures in both builds (0.06 and 0.33 levels apart on average), yet R1 went from 9 to 8 and R2 from pass to fail with other critics and jurors; R3 went from 6 to 5 (parts 7 and 5, round 16 had 6 and 6); R4 stays 6 with round 16's terrain major gone (the near clouds now slide about 410 px against the horizon across the view, round 16 about 70) and the covers changed from two horizons to one. Summary in `scorecards/round-023/`.
- Round 24, Phase 4, sun, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 18:20 UTC. Judged on main at 530168f. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 5, R4 6, R8 pass. 0 severe, 11 major. Next: Sun has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Near surface in the terrain view, 0 to 90 (two R3 majors): the granules closest to the camera are soft, blurry and smeared toward the corners, with none of the lanes and bright points the middle ground keeps. (2) Far photosphere at 60 (R3 major): mirror symmetric butterfly seams repeat in the granulation just below the horizon, between the sunspot and the limb. (3) Limb and sky in the terrain view (two R3 majors): the orange glow above the spicules ends in a hard, dark rimmed curved line with a doubled shell edge, and a ruler straight bright horizontal line crosses the foot of the left loop and runs on into the sky. (4) Rise cover, 196 to 222 (two R4 majors): from 211 to 216 the sunspot and its rim tighten and streak toward one point faster than the surface around them recedes, so the cover reads as a radial warp and not as a pullback, and the tall arch is bright at 204 and gone as a shape by 211. (5) Granulation as a material (two R3 majors): at 247 it reads as glossy poured fluid that pinches into the pores, and on the whole disc at 360 it is one even, busy, painterly noise with no change of scale toward the limb and sunspots that share one starburst shape. (6) Corona at 360 (R3 major): a fan of straight brush stroke streaks of nearly equal length and spacing all round the disc, some of them in front of the stars. (7) Pack coverage (R4 major): the critic asks for a dive sheet; the Sun has no dive, because the film opens on its surface (`kit/01-FILM.md`), so no change to the film answers this one. Minors: the loop feet a little smaller and dimmer than approved and the grade a touch redder at 90, sky and streamer detail about 7 percent softer at 360, a jagged pure black ribbon between the warm glow and the stars and stretched corner stars at frame 0, a wet highlight on the cell crowns at 90, flat hard edged prominences, a thin bright outline round the disc and a smudgy Milky Way with stars of one size at 360, and an arch that keeps the same filament pattern from 0 to 98. Against round 15: R1 went from 8 to 9, R3 from 3 to 5 (parts 5, 7 and 7), R4 from 4 to 6, and the jury from 0 of 10 to 2 of 10 against 2 of 10 for the approved images. All four severe issues of round 15 are gone: the kaleidoscope seams behind the sunspot are down to one major, the vertical tile seam in the Milky Way at 247 and the cut through a whiteout at the rise cover are not reported, and the black moat round the glow is down to a minor. The grey blotches in the corona and the swimming granulation of the terrain view are not reported either. Summary in `scorecards/round-024/`.
- Round 25, Phase 4, jupiter, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 18:20 UTC. Judged on main at 530168f. Measures green. R1 10, R2 pass, R3 7, R4 5, R8 pass. 0 severe, 5 major. Next: Jupiter has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Rise cover, 3265 to 3276 (two R4 majors): the cover ends on a flat beige disc with no bands, then the bands, the fine detail and the star field come in within two frames and the colour goes from grey beige to bright cream by 3280; and at 3270 to 3271 the limb goes from a shallow slope to a steep arc in one frame, which reads as a snap in roll and not as one move. (2) Sky in both covers (R4 major): the stars and the Milky Way drop to pure black from 3021 to 3026 and from 3267 to 3274 and come back after. (3) Dive 3002 to 3010 (R4 major): after a nearly still hold the globe grows from half the frame to all of it in eight frames, a lurch; the end of the rise, 3274 to 3287, stops as abruptly. (4) Terrain 3150, the near cloud folds below and right of the storm (R3 major): neighbouring folds curl into smooth hollow tubes, the lit tips keep detail and the broad faces between them look smeared and sculpted. Minors: the south limb falls from cloud brightness to space in four to six pixels at 2895 and 2997, Io is softer and a little glossy beside the cloud deck and reads slightly as a cutout at 3296, far bands in the terrain view flatten into long parallel streaks, the blue glow in the dark foreground reads as an overlay, a few fuzzy elongated stars at the lower left of 3296, stepped copies of bright edges in the blur at 3019 and 3265 to 3267, and little parallax between the near cloud tops and the horizon across the terrain view. Against round 17: R1 went from 7 to 10 (the hold sky is back to one texel per pixel and Io's night limb and shadow are back to the approved image), R3 stays 7 (parts 8 and 7), R4 went from 4 to 5; the severe of round 17 is gone (the rise now keeps the bands near level from its first clear frame), as are the majors on the tone step after the dive cover, on the exposure climb before the rise cover and on the flat terrain push (now a minor). R2 passes with 3 of 10 against 2 of 10 for the approved images. Summary in `scorecards/round-025/`.
- Round 26, Phase 4, saturn, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 18:48 UTC. Judged on main at 00c1f81. Measures green. R1 6, R2 fail, R3 7, R4 4, R8 pass. 1 severe, 13 major. Next: Saturn has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Terrain view, 3663 to 3744 (R4 severe): the lower border of the warped close image enters the frame as a sloped seam with clamped, smeared ring stripes below it, and the outer ring edge at the upper left tears sideways into space. (2) Terrain 3720 against the approved close image (four R1 majors): the rings end in a hard vertical step and a dark notch at the sunlit limb, the limb glow rises late and stops in a hard edge, and the fine grooves carry about 30 percent less edge energy with the rings brighter and flatter, worst at the far left. (3) Hold 3465 (two R1 majors): the faint translucent streak over the rings is gone, the limb is brighter and softer, the ring divisions sit off the approved radii and the bands are warmer and brighter. (4) Dive and rise (three R4 majors): the dive swings Saturn to the upper right, stalls near 3600 and turns back down and left under the cover (3597 to 3600) while the Milky Way fades; the rise pushes Saturn right from 3817 to 3830 with a notch at the lit left limb at 3823 while the Milky Way comes in out of black, then whips Saturn left to 3857 without receding further. (5) Rings at 3720 and 3866 (two R3 majors): single pixel white and pink specks over the lit ring surface that read as render noise, not as ring particles. (6) Globe (two R3 majors): the fine cloud texture drops into broad soft brown bands across the terminator at 3567, and at 3866 the bands are soft and airbrushed beside the crisp rings. The jury failed the stop by one vote: 2 of 10 against 5 of 10 for the approved images, where 3 of 10 passes. Minors: rings a little warmer and brighter and the upper left limb a few pixels off at 3465, lifted blacks in the terrain sky, smooth nested ovals in the blue polar cap with the amber rim as a separate line, a scratchy speckled finish on the bright ring tracks at 3465, a straight soft glow across the stars at the lower right of 3567, Titan nearly featureless at 3465 and a flat disc at 3866, the nearest ring texture as stretched grain at 3720, the limb glow as a wide even halo at 3720, a faint thin arc outside the rings and a smooth blocky sky gradient at 3866, the pan speeding up after 3837 under the rise cover, and the clouds standing still through the terrain view with a faint smear outside the lower left limb by 3809. Against round 20: R1 went from 7 to 6, R3 from 4 to 7 (parts 7 and 7), R4 from 8 to 4, and the jury from 3 of 10 to 2 of 10. The three severe seams of round 20 are gone (the angular notch and the clipped sliver of the shadowed outer ring and the clipped streak at 3465, the detached crescent at the lower left limb of 3567), as are the white discs on the rings at 3720. The camera plan is the same file in both builds but for the sky aim, so the dive and rise that R4 marks down here are round 20's as well. Summary in `scorecards/round-026/`.
- Round 27, Phase 4, earth, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 20:28 UTC. Judged on main at cb86bfe. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 6, R4 4, R8 pass. 1 severe, 9 major. Next: Earth has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Dive handover, 1870 to 1879 (R4 severe): the blurred whole Earth with the Moon and the continents is replaced by a canted cloud limb; scale, roll and exposure do not carry, so the arrival is not a blur of the same view. (2) Rise cover, 2129 to 2132 (R4 major): the rise stalls, then the horizon leaps about 25 pixels and leaves in one frame while the top of the frame flips from black space to white. (3) Dive cover, 1884 to 1890 (R4 major): the roll stops within a frame and the plate stays locked while the fog thins, so the near cloud and the horizon do not move against each other. (4) Night limb at 1755, 1857 and 2156 (three R3 majors): a doubled edge, a thin bright rim a few pixels inside a second dark rim, at 1857 a grey arc apart from the globe edge, at 2156 a sharp thin outer shell with a dark gap inside a faint outer haze. (5) Aurora at 1755 and 1857 (two R3 majors): a row of repeated, evenly spaced green comb strokes on the limb with a flat red smudge behind it and a hard inner edge; it reads as a sticker and not as light in the air. (6) Sky at 2156 (R3 major): the stars in the dense band are blurry, smeared blobs and blocky patches. (7) Moon at 2156 (R3 major): the maria and craters are soft and airbrushed at native size and the lit left edge is a jagged, stepped rim. Minors: against the approved images the green knots on the night side are dimmed and the curtain more diffuse, the left glow a touch paler with a thin cyan outline on the lower arc, the Moon a little dim, the horizon band of the terrain view paler and the cloud shadows a little warm and flat (R1); stretched cloud striations at the southern limb, airbrushed and oversharpened land with bright coastlines, the globe cut by the frame at 1857, city lights as uniform dots and as blobs with blocky halos, painterly and crunchy land and cloud tops at 2156, tonal stepping in the upper sky of the terrain view and a colour smudge under one coastal cloud (R3). Against round 19: R1 held at 9 with six minors where there were eight, R3 went from 5 to 6 (parts 6 and 6), R4 stayed at 4, and the jury went from 1 of 10 to 2 of 10 against 2 of 10 for the approved images. Not reported any more: the ghost copies in the dive (round 19's severe), the rise that shrank the globe in seven frames and froze on another face with the Moon gone, the Moon sliding across locked stars in the hold, the missing terminator and the cutout night limb with no glow, Greenland as a chalky patch and the city lights out in the Atlantic. Still reported: the comb aurora and the soft stars in the band at 2156. New: the severe at the dive handover, both cover majors, and the doubled shell on the night limb that came with its new glow. Summary in `scorecards/round-027/`.
- Round 28, Phase 4, uranus, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 21:30 UTC. Judged on main at ae11e38. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 4, R4 7, R8 pass. 1 severe, 7 major. Next: Uranus has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Dive frame 4137, surface (R3 severe): the close-up planet texture is heavily stretched and blurry with directional smearing across the cyan bands, and the white clouds break into horizontal rectangular pixel blocks and stepped streaks. (2) Dive frame 4137, limb (R3 major): regular horizontal ribs like scanlines and jagged pixel steps cut into the outer horizon edge and the bright cyan white limb gradient. (3) Dive frame 4137, rings (R3 major): the thin inner ringlets pinch together, wobble and fold in wavy ripples, and the brighter upper left band shows diagonal ribbed pixelation. (4) Terrain view at 4290 (two R3 majors): the sunlit cloud decks are diagonally stretched and airbrushed, soft smeared bands and not sharp filaments or cells, and the near surface in the lower frame is a blurred wash, one faint smudge over a flat gradient. (5) Rise frame 4436 (R3 major): the disc is vertically brushed and nearly featureless beside sharp ring shadows, so the surface looks painted, and the polar wisps are softer than those shadows. (6) Dive cover, 4168 to 4170 (R4 major): the crisp cyan limb and the long ring arcs arrive almost together between consecutive frames while the broad cloud band is still in view, so the two layers read as a reveal and not as one camera move. (7) Rise, 4414 to 4416 (R4 major): a distinct curved limb shows beneath the still diagonal streak of air, so the change of horizon is seen before the cover clears. Minors: against the approved images the blacks of the terrain view a touch lifted and the grade slightly brighter and cooler, cloud streaks a little softer with a slightly milky night side, the dust knot in the Milky Way smoother, the lit limb glow a hair brighter and wider (R1); the Milky Way soft and slightly blocky beside crisp stars, single white specks on the sunlit globe and a stepped night limb, corner stars stretched into ovals with faint rainbow smudges, the central disc at 4436 an airbrushed gradient, the night side of the terrain view a flat fill (R3); the globe's cloud knots barely move in the hold while the moons drift (R4). Against round 21: R1 went from 7 to 9, R4 from 4 to 7, R3 fell from 6 to 4 (parts 4 and 6), and the jury went from 0 of 10 to 2 of 10 against 1 of 10 for the approved images. Not reported any more: both severe issues of the rise (the frames after the cover were not one move, and the camera did not come back to the place it left), the jump in position and direction before the dive cover, the step in speed between 4118 and 4126, the ghost copies in both covers, the thick flat ring strokes and the line along the disc edge at 4290, the larger right moon with its cut out rim. Still reported: the brushed, streaked surface at 4137, 4290 and 4436, now with pixel blocks in the clouds at 4137 (severe), and a join at the end of the dive cover, down from a pop of sun and rings within three frames to one major. New: the ribs on the limb and the rippled ringlets at 4137, and the limb seen under the cover at 4414 to 4416. Summary in `scorecards/round-028/`.
- Round 29, Phase 4, mercury, round 3 of 3. Started 2026-11-21 22:29 UTC. Judged on main at 8c9aa59. Measures green. R1 9, R2 pass, R3 7, R4 4, R8 pass. 2 severe, 8 major. Next: Mercury has used its three rounds, so nothing follows; what stays open, worst first. (1) Rise, 994 to 1014 (R4 severe): the departing globe does not match the place the camera left; the grey terrain at 994 pops to a warm brown, blue flecked crater field at 1000, the Sun vanishes, and from 1014 the globe is lit from the upper left with its dark limb lower right, while the hold had the Sun at upper right lighting the right limb. (2) Dive, 722 to 743 (R4 severe): the cover is not one move; the Sun jumps from upper right (722 to 730) to top centre on the horizon (739 on), the warm tan and blue globe becomes neutral grey terrain, and the curve of the limb changes abruptly from 739 to 743. (3) Dive frame 717 (R3 major): a narrow cyan streak cuts across crater rims and does not follow the ground. (4) Terrain view at 870 (R3 major): fine relief on the walls and the near rim of the large crater smears into long parallel ribbons while the crater floor stays much sharper. (5) Rise frame 1016 (R3 major): broad milky patches wash out the small craters and several soft white spots look like glows painted over the sharper rock. (6) Arrival in the terrain, 741 to 743 (R4 major): heavy motion blur through 742 stops dead at 743, the camera goes from high speed to almost still in one frame. (7) Start of the rise, 991 to 993 (R4 major): the camera is nearly still at 991 and fully blurred at 992 and 993, with no ease into the departure. (8) Dive frame 730 (R4 major): the zoom blur smears the crater texture into radial streaks of repeated crater copies, a stretched texture and not a smooth blur. (9) Terrain view, 780 to 959 (R4 major): the camera hardly travels, near ground and horizon shift together with little parallax, so the view reads as a flat slow pan and not as flight over relief. (10) The Sun at the horizon, 743 to 780 (R4 major): the star burst flare of 743 to 779 has collapsed to a dim glow by 780 with neither camera nor Sun moving much, so the exposure changes. Minors: against the approved images the rim highlight of the lower right sunlit edge slightly duller with a tighter glow, the faint horizontal flare streak and the warm veil over the upper left dark side weaker, the sunlit rock of the terrain view a touch cooler and dimmer with the finest terrace ridges slightly smoothed (R1); faint rectangular flare ghosts on the dark disc at 615 and 717, a narrow warm halo along the bright edge of the globe at 1016, near white pinpricks that read as glitter on the foreground plains at 870 (R3); small bright specks in the black crater floor that shimmer from frame to frame, and an end of the rise whose sky is almost pure black with few stars (R4). Against round 18: R1 went from 8 to 9, R3 from 6 to 7, R4 from 3 to 4, the jury from 4 of 10 (fail) to 6 of 10 against 7 of 10 for the approved images (pass), severe issues from four to two, majors from seven to eight. Not reported any more: both whiteout flashes and the one frame pops they hid (743 to 744, 989 to 990), the stepped ghost copies of the pullback at 1000 to 1007 and its abrupt stop, the smeared crater rims and the chunky limb at 717, the crushed black crater shadow with its floating speck at 870, the flat blue grey night disc with its cut out edge at 1016, the planet and terrain sitting 1 to 2 px off the approved framing, the warm bias of the Sun, the rectangular edge of the solar glare in the hold. Still reported: the frames before and after the dive cover are not one move (the Sun's place, the colour of the ground), the rise does not come back to the light of the hold, the fast zoom at 730 breaks the craters into copies, glitter specks on the plains, flare ghosts on the dark disc. New: the cyan streak at 717, the ribbons on the crater walls where round 18 read painted swirls, the milky patches at 1016 (a minor in round 18, a major now), the missing ease at both ends of the terrain view, the terrain view read as a flat slow pan, the flare that changes between 779 and 780. Summary in `scorecards/round-029/`.
- Round 30, Phase 5, whole film, round 1 of 3. Started 2026-11-22 04:39 UTC. Judged on main at 2621f00. R5 5, R6 7, R7 8, R2 pass, R3 4, R4 5. 3 severe, 21 major.    
    ```
    MARS READY FOR REVIEW
    Review page: https://grand-tour-film.pplx.app/review.html
    Watch it live: https://grand-tour-film.pplx.app?mode=autopilot&stop=mars
    Fly it: https://grand-tour-film.pplx.app?stop=mars
    Side by side: out/stills/compare-mars-hold.jpg and out/stills/compare-mars-terrain.jpg
    Rounds run: 3 of 5
    Scores: R1 7, R2 pass, R3 5, R4 6, R8 pass
    Measures: G5 pass, G13 pass, G17 pass
    Open items: ten, numbers 6 to 15 under "Open items" in kit/PROGRESS.md. The climb out reads as a concealed cut where the dust clears (frames 2695 to 2706). The canyon resolves almost in place as the dust of the dive thins (2456 to 2460). The sunlit limb is a hard stepped edge and the night limb is razor sharp (2325). Speckle near the limb and stepped rings on the polar cap (2325). The storm spiral reads as painted (2325, 2427). In the pullback the far side is smeared, the cap is blown out and there is no soft terminator (2726). The shadowed canyon ground is muddy and the horizon glow is a flat hot band, both as in the approved image (2580). Minors: the moon leaves the dive too fast, its edge is stepped and it is softer than the planet, the foreground rock is slightly soft. Carried: the pullback shows Mars lit from below with north to the left, the turn toward Jupiter banks about 90 degrees, and the moon is not in the pullback.
    What I would improve next: The two handovers first: carry the speed of the plunge into the first second of the canyon view, and let the dust of the climb stream with the camera and thin over ten frames or more so the limb grows into view.
    Then the limb: one haze layer drawn across the edge of the disc, and a faint blue rim on the night side.
    Then the pullback: repaint the far side of the map at the detail of the dive patches, rebuild the cap on a polar projection and lower its exposure.
    To continue: send the resume prompt from kit/10-KICKOFF.md
    ```
